<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds that are homotopy equivalent but not homeomorphic, disproving the homeomorphism-existence formulation of the Borel conjecture in dimension four. Their common fundamental group is word-hyperbolic. One of the manifolds also has a self-homotopy equivalence not homotopic to a homeomorphism.

- **claim-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pair)): There exist closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds $M$ and $N$ that are homotopy equivalent but not homeomorphic. Their common fundamental group is word-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: Homotopy-equivalent, non-homeomorphic closed aspherical four-manifolds with word-hyperbolic group.
- **claim-self-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-self-map)): There exist a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold $M$ and a homotopy equivalence $f\colon M\to M$ such that no homeomorphism $M\to M$ is homotopic to $f$.
  - Explanation: A self-homotopy equivalence not homotopic to any homeomorphism.
- **claim-cyclic-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cyc-criterion)): Let $Q$ be a connected finite aspherical CW complex, put $G=\pi_1(Q)$, and let $w\colon G\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z$ be an epimorphism. For every positive integer $d$, write $$G_d=w^{-1}(d\mathbb Z) \qquad\text{and}\qquad Q_d\longrightarrow Q$$ for the associated connected cyclic cover. Fix an integer $n\geq1$. Suppose that $Q_2$ has a closed topological $n$-manifold model $M$, but that $Q_d$ has no such model for every sufficiently large odd integer $d$.* *Choose $\gamma\in G$ with $w(\gamma)=1$, and use the model equivalence to identify $\pi_1(M)$ with $G_2$. The outer automorphism of $G_2$ represented by $$\alpha(g)=\gamma g\gamma^{-1}$$ is induced by a self-homotopy equivalence of $M$. No homeomorphism of $M$ induces this outer automorphism. In particular, that self-homotopy equivalence is not homotopic to a homeomorphism.
  - Explanation: A model for the double cover and none for large odd covers give a non-realizable outer automorphism.
- **claim-homotopy-involution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cyc-homotopy-involution)): The equivalence $f$ in Theorem <a href="#cyc:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:criterion">3</a> satisfies $f^2\simeq\operatorname{id}_M$. Indeed, $\alpha^2=\operatorname{Ad}_{\gamma^2}$ is inner because $\gamma^2\in G_2$, and unbased homotopy classes of maps between aspherical CW-type spaces are classified by homomorphisms modulo inner automorphisms of the target. The proof nevertheless imposes no order condition on a hypothetical realizing homeomorphism. Its only geometric input is the bounded height displacement of a lift commuting with the deck translation.
  - Explanation: The self-equivalence squares to the identity up to homotopy.
- **claim-sylow-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-criterion)): Let $X$ and $Y$ be connected topological manifolds whose fundamental groups are marked by a group $\Pi$, and suppose that $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ is finite. Let $\mathsf A,\mathsf B\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ be $2$-subgroups of the same order. Suppose that there is an involution $\delta$ for which $$N_{\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)}(\mathsf A)=\mathsf A\times\langle\delta\rangle$$ is an internal direct product, and that $$\mathsf A\leq R_X,\qquad \mathsf B\leq R_Y, \qquad \delta\notin R_X.$$ If $\mathsf A$ and $\mathsf B$ are not conjugate in $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$, then $X$ and $Y$ are not homeomorphic.
  - Explanation: Non-conjugate realized two-subgroups with the stated normalizer prevent a homeomorphism.
- **claim-reflection-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-refl-input)): Let $D$ be a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold with connected boundary $S$ and free fundamental group $H$. Suppose that $D$ has the homotopy type of a finite graph with $2k$ two-spheres, and that its whole middle module $H_2(\widetilde D;\mathbb Z)$ over $\mathbb ZH$ has a specified framed immersed hyperbolic basis of $k$ planes, with vanishing quadratic self-intersections. Attach three-cells along the basis spheres to obtain a map of collared pairs $$(D,S)\longrightarrow(P,S)$$ that is the identity on $S$ and induces the given identification of fundamental groups. Assume that $(P,S)$ is an oriented Poincaré pair of dimension four, that $P\simeq BH$, and that $\pi_1(S)\to H$ is surjective.* *For a finite flag triangulation of $S$, let $Q=\mathcal R_A(P)$ be <a href="#refl:finite-reflection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="refl:finite-reflection">[refl:finite-reflection]</a>. Then $Q$ is a finite aspherical oriented Poincaré complex of dimension four. If $Q$ has a closed topological four-manifold model, then $S$ admits a marked graph-type filling: there is a compact oriented topological four-manifold $V$ with an orientation-preserving boundary identification $\partial V=S$ and a homotopy equivalence $V\to P$ whose boundary restriction is homotopic to the given inclusion $S\to P$.
  - Explanation: A manifold model of the reflected complex yields a marked filling of the chamber boundary.
- **claim-parity-chamber** (construction, [source](#stmt-refl-chamber-data)): There are chamber data $(D,P,S,H)$ satisfying the chamber hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#refl:input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="refl:input">6</a>, with $H$ a finitely generated nonabelian free group, and a distinguished epimorphism $w\colon H\to\mathbb Z$ with the following properties. For $d\geq1$, put $H_d=w^{-1}(d\mathbb Z)$, let $D_d,P_d$ be the associated connected cyclic covers, and let $S_d$ be the preimage of $S$.* 1. *For every $d$, the boundary $S_d$ is connected, and $(D_d,P_d,S_d,H_d)$ satisfies all the chamber hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#refl:input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="refl:input">6</a>, with the full framed immersed hyperbolic basis and its vanishing quadratic self-intersections lifted from $D$.* 2. *There is a compact oriented topological four-manifold $V$ with an orientation-preserving boundary identification $\partial V=S_2$ and a homotopy equivalence $$j:(V,S_2)\longrightarrow(P_2,S_2)$$ equal to the identity on the boundary.* 3. *For every odd $d\geq3$, there is no compact oriented topological four-manifold $V_d$ with an orientation-preserving boundary identification $\partial V_d=S_d$ and a homotopy equivalence $V_d\to P_d$ whose boundary restriction is homotopic to the inclusion $S_d\to P_d$.
  - Explanation: Chamber data with a filling in the double cover and none in odd covers.
- **claim-lifted-flag** (theorem, [source](#stmt-refl-cover-comparison)): The triangulation $\mathcal L_d$ is flag. Let $\mathsf A_d=(\mathbb Z/2)^{\mathcal V_d}$, where $\mathcal V_d$ is its vertex set, and distinguish all its lifted panels in the reflection $$\widehat Q_d=\mathcal R_{\mathsf A_d}(P_d).$$ There is a connected finite covering $\widehat Q_d\to Q^0_d$.
  - Explanation: The lifted triangulation is flag and a finite cover separates panel labels.
- **claim-full-odd-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-refl-odd-models)): For every odd integer $d\geq3$, the space $Q^0_d$ has no closed topological four-manifold model.
  - Explanation: No odd cover of the full reflection has a closed four-manifold model.
- **claim-rigid-relations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-rigid-relations)): The subspace $\mathsf C$ has dimension eight, and every nonzero element of $\mathsf C$ has weight at least eight. If a simplicial automorphism $\sigma$ of $\mathcal L$ has $\sigma_*(\mathsf C)=\mathsf C$, where $\sigma_*(e_s)=e_{\sigma(s)}$, then $\sigma$ is the identity.
  - Explanation: The relation space has large minimum weight and rigidifies simplicial symmetries.
- **claim-quotient-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-quotient-cover)): The map $q$ is a regular finite covering with deck group $\mathsf C$. The space $Q$ is a connected finite aspherical CW complex. The folding map $$\label{mod:folding} r:Q\longrightarrow P,\qquad r([b,x])=x,$$ is split by the inclusion of any chamber.
  - Explanation: The label quotient is a regular finite covering onto an aspherical finite complex.
- **claim-quotient-odd-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-odd-models)): For every odd integer $d\geq3$, the space $Q_d$ has no closed topological four-manifold model.
  - Explanation: The quotient reflection has no manifold model in odd covers.
- **claim-panel-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-panel-coordinates)): Let $y\in S_2$, and enumerate $J_K(y)=\{u_1,\ldots,u_q\}$. Then $1\leq q\leq4$. A neighborhood of $(y,0)$ in $S_2\times[0,\infty)$ has coordinates in a relative-open neighborhood of the origin in $\mathbb R^{4-q}\times[0,\infty)^q$ such that the lifted panel $p_2^{-1}(S^K_{u_i})\times\{0\}$ is the face $x_i=0$. The neighborhood may be chosen to meet no lifted panel with label outside $J_K(y)$.
  - Explanation: Orthant coordinates identify lifted panels with coordinate faces.
- **claim-doubled-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-models)): Let $K$ be a finite PL triangulation of $S$. Let $\mathsf F$ be a finite-dimensional $\mathbb F_2$-vector space with labels $(\lambda_u)_{u\in\mathcal U}$ spanning $\mathsf F$ and linearly independent on every simplex of $K$. Using the fixed relative filling $j:(V,S_2)\to(P_2,S_2)$, for every linear map $\epsilon:\mathsf F\to\mathbb F_2$ the space $\mathcal M_\epsilon$ defined by <a href="#mod:boundary-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:boundary-relation">[mod:boundary-relation]</a> is a closed connected topological four-manifold, and $\varphi_\epsilon:\mathcal M_\epsilon\to R_2$ in <a href="#mod:comparison-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:comparison-map">[mod:comparison-map]</a> is a homotopy equivalence. If $R_2$ is aspherical, then $\mathcal M_\epsilon$ is aspherical.
  - Explanation: Twisted gluing of fillings gives a closed manifold homotopy equivalent to the doubled reflection.
- **claim-even-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-refl-even-model)): For every finite flag triangulation $K$ of $S$, let $Q^0(K)$ be its full reflection, using one independent basis label for each vertex. The connected double cyclic cover of $Q^0(K)$ defined by the folding character has a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold model.
  - Explanation: The double cover of a full reflection has an aspherical manifold model.
- **claim-model-actions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-actions)): The action <a href="#mod:ambient-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:ambient-action">[mod:ambient-action]</a> is faithful. For each linear $\epsilon:\mathsf A\to\mathbb F_2$, its restriction to $\mathsf B_\epsilon$ is realized by a faithful action by homeomorphisms on $M_\epsilon$, and $f_\epsilon$ is equivariant for these actions. Under the marking by $\Pi$, the outer automorphism induced by each of these homeomorphisms is the one induced by the corresponding action on $Q_2$.
  - Explanation: Graph subgroups act faithfully by homeomorphisms on the models, equivariantly.
- **claim-lifted-nerve** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-lifted-nerve)): The complex $\widehat{\mathcal L}$ is a connected, locally finite, flag-no-square PL triangulation of the three-manifold $\widehat S$. The deck action of $H$ is free on its vertices, and its quotient is $\mathcal L$. In particular, each vertex link is a PL two-sphere.
  - Explanation: The lift keeps the flag and no-square properties.
- **claim-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-support)): Let $W(\mathcal G)$ be a right-angled Coxeter group on an arbitrary simple graph with vertex set $V$. For $J\subseteq V$, the special subgroup $W_J$ generated by $J$ is the right-angled Coxeter group on the induced graph, and $$W_J\cap W_K=W_{J\cap K}.$$ Every $d\in W(\mathcal G)$ has a unique smallest vertex set $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(d)$ for which $d\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(d)}$. This support is finite, and graph automorphisms transport supports.
  - Explanation: Special subgroups have the intersection formula and unique finite supports.
- **claim-development** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-development)): The space $\widetilde Q$ is contractible. If $\overline x$ and $x_2$ are the images of $x$ in $P$ and $P_2$, respectively, the maps $$\begin{aligned} [c,x]&\longmapsto[\chi_0(c),\overline x]\in Q^0,\\ [c,x]&\longmapsto[\chi(c),\overline x]\in Q,\\ [c,x]&\longmapsto[\chi(c),x_2]\in Q_2 \end{aligned}$$ are universal coverings. With the chosen deck-group convention, $$\label{ext:groups} \pi_1(Q^0)=\ker\chi_0,\qquad G:=\pi_1(Q)=\ker\chi,\qquad \Pi:=\pi_1(Q_2)=\ker\eta .$$ The homomorphism $w\colon G\to\mathbb Z$ induced by the folding map is $w$ composed with the projection $\mathcal E\to H$.
  - Explanation: The development is contractible and identifies the fundamental groups as kernels.
- **claim-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-embedding)): Conjugation gives an injection $\mathcal E\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Pi)$. It identifies $\Pi$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Inn}}(\Pi)$ and induces an inclusion $$\label{ext:outer} \mathcal E/\Pi =\mathsf A\times\langle\delta\rangle \ \leq\ \mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi), \qquad \delta=(0,1).$$ Here $\langle\delta\rangle\cong\mathbb F_2$. Inside $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Pi)$, $\mathcal E$ is the full preimage of the product in <a href="#ext:outer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:outer">[ext:outer]</a>; $\mathcal E_2$ and $G$ are the full preimages of its factors $\mathsf A$ and $\langle\delta\rangle$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The extension embeds in the automorphism group with the label-and-deck quotient in Out.
- **claim-outer-actions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-outer-actions)): Under <a href="#ext:outer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:outer">[ext:outer]</a>, the action of $\mathcal E/\Pi$ on $Q_2$ is the label-translation and cyclic-deck action $$(a,t)[b,y]=[a+b,\theta^t y], \qquad (a,t)\in\mathsf A\times\mathbb F_2.$$ With the markings supplied by Proposition <a href="#mod:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:models">14</a>, the graph action on $M_\epsilon$ from Proposition <a href="#mod:actions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:actions">16</a> induces exactly the subgroup $$\mathsf B_\epsilon =\{(a,\epsilon(a)):a\in\mathsf A\} \leq\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi).$$ In particular, every element of $\mathsf A=\mathsf B_0$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $M$, and every element of $\mathsf B_\ell$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $N$.
  - Explanation: Homeomorphisms of the models induce exactly the two graph subgroups.
- **claim-delta-unrealized** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-delta)): With the marking $\pi_1(M)\cong\Pi$, no homeomorphism of $M$ induces the outer automorphism $\delta$.
  - Explanation: No homeomorphism of the first model induces the cyclic involution.
- **claim-nonconjugate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-nonconjugate)): The subgroups $\mathsf A$ and $\mathsf B_\ell$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ are not conjugate.
  - Explanation: The two subgroups differ in elements with finite-order lifts, hence are not conjugate.
- **claim-cubical-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-hyp-cubical-model)): There are a locally finite tree $T$ with a free cocompact $H$-action, an $H$-equivariant family of finite subtrees $(T_u)_{u\in\widehat{\mathcal V}}$, and a locally finite CAT(0) cube complex $X$ with a proper cocompact $\mathcal E$-action, with the following properties:* 1. *If $u,u'$ are adjacent in $\widehat{\mathcal L}$, then $T_u$ and $T_{u'}$ meet at a vertex. Moreover, $$b_T:=\sup_{u\in\widehat{\mathcal V}}\|T_u^{(0)}\|<\infty.$$* 2. *The vertices of $X$ are the pairs $(a,t)\in W\times T^{(0)}$. Its edges are exactly $$\begin{aligned} (a,t)&\longleftrightarrow(au,t) &&\text{if }u\in\widehat{\mathcal V}\text{ and }t\in T_u^{(0)},\\ (a,t)&\longleftrightarrow(a,t') &&\text{if }[t,t']\text{ is an edge of }T. \end{aligned} \label{hyp:edge-graph}$$* 3. *On these vertices the action is $$(b,h)(a,t)=(b\,h(a),ht) \qquad (b\in W,\ h\in H).$$ The cubes of $X$ have dimension at most five.
  - Explanation: A tree of Davis complexes gives a proper cocompact cubical model.
- **claim-bounded-grids** (bound, [source](#stmt-hyp-bounded-grids)): Every isometric embedding of a square grid of side length $n$ into $X^{(1)}$ satisfies $$n\leq 16b_T-1.$$
  - Explanation: Isometric square grids have uniformly bounded side length.
- **claim-grids-triangles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hyp-grids-to-triangles)): Let $Y$ be a CAT(0) cube complex. Suppose every isometrically embedded square grid in its edge graph has side length at most $K$, where $K$ is a nonnegative integer. Then any two vertices of $I(x,y)$ at the same distance from $x$ are at distance at most $2K$, uniformly in $x,y$. Every geodesic triangle with vertex corners in the edge graph is $4K$-thin at its vertices.
  - Explanation: Bounded grids imply uniformly thin triangles in the edge graph.
- **claim-word-hyperbolic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hyp-word-hyperbolic)): The groups $\mathcal E$ and $\Pi$ are word-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: The extension and the manifold group are word-hyperbolic.
- **claim-no-vc-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hyp-no-vc-splitting)): The group $\Pi$ admits no nontrivial splitting over a virtually cyclic subgroup. It has one end.
  - Explanation: Duality and Shapiro's lemma exclude virtually cyclic splittings.
- **claim-finite-out** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hyp-finite-out)): The outer automorphism group $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ is finite.
  - Explanation: The outer automorphism group is finite.
- **claim-coxeter-facts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nor-coxeter-facts)): Let $W(\mathcal G)$ be the right-angled Coxeter group on a simple graph $\mathcal G$ with vertex set $V$.* 1. *For $u\in V$, $$C_{W(\mathcal G)}(u)=W_{\mathop{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathcal G}(u)}.$$* 2. *Every finite-order element is conjugate to a product of distinct generators supported on a clique, with the empty product allowed.* 3. *The center is the special subgroup on the universal vertices: $$Z(W(\mathcal G)) =W_{\{v\in V:\ v\text{ is adjacent to every }u\ne v\}}.$$
  - Explanation: Centralizers, finite-order elements and centers of right-angled Coxeter groups.
- **claim-generator-lines** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nor-generator-lines)): Every automorphism of $W$ induces a permutation of the coordinate vectors in $$W_{\mathrm{ab}} =\bigoplus_{u\in\widehat{\mathcal V}}\mathbb F_2\,\mathbf e_u.$$ The resulting vertex permutation is a simplicial automorphism of $\widehat{\mathcal L}$. After composing with the inverse of that generator permutation, the original automorphism sends each Coxeter generator to a conjugate of itself.
  - Explanation: Automorphisms permute coordinate vectors and induce simplicial symmetries.
- **claim-conjugating** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nor-conjugating)): Let $X$ be $\widehat{\mathcal L}$ or the link in $\widehat{\mathcal L}$ of a simplex of dimension $0$, $1$, or $2$. If an automorphism $\varphi$ of $W(X)$ sends each vertex generator to a conjugate of itself, then $\varphi$ is inner.
  - Explanation: Generator-conjugating automorphisms are inner.
- **claim-coxeter-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nor-rigidity)): Every automorphism of $W$ is a composite of an inner automorphism and an automorphism induced by a simplicial automorphism of $\widehat{\mathcal L}$.
  - Explanation: Automorphisms are inner composed with simplicial ones.
- **claim-normalizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nor-normalizer)): Inside $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$, $$\label{nor:normalizer-equality} N_{\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)}(\mathsf A) =\mathsf A\times\langle\delta\rangle.$$
  - Explanation: The normalizer of the label subgroup is the label subgroup times the involution.
- **claim-four-tip-body** (construction, [source](#stmt-ch-four-tip-model)): The four-tip body has a three-dimensional handlebody thickening that, with its four marked tip annuli, is a boundary connected sum of four solid tori with longitudinal marked annuli. The finite list above can be placed with disjoint bodies and the markings <a href="#ch:tip-markings" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ch:tip-markings">[ch:tip-markings]</a> in the outside pieces of the plumbing. The bottom surfaces are proper inward pushes of disjoint boundary surfaces of $X$. Each terminal cap is embedded and framed; it misses all body sheets away from its own attachment. The only possible cap–cap intersections occur between opposite ports of a plumbing.
  - Explanation: The four-tip body has a marked handlebody thickening and disjoint placement.
- **claim-boundary-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-laws)): For each installed body, its initial boundary represents, up to overall conjugation, a word $L$ in the four based tips satisfying $$\label{ch:law-properties} L\big\|_{X_\alpha=1}=1\quad(1\leq\alpha\leq4), \qquad L\equiv [[X_1,X_2],[X_3,X_4]] \pmod{\Gamma_5 F(X_1,X_2,X_3,X_4)}.$$ Here $\Gamma_r$ is the lower central series, and orientations are chosen for the displayed sign.
  - Explanation: Boundary words are nested commutators modulo higher terms.
- **claim-exterior-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-exterior-model)): There is a homotopy-pushout model $$\label{ch:exterior-pushout} E\simeq X\cup_{\coprod_jF_j}\coprod_j(F_j\times S^1),$$ where $F_j$ maps to $F_j\times S^1$ by its inward section. Under this model the displayed maps from $F_j\times S^1$ are the actual tube-side maps, and $S^1$ is the normal circle.
  - Explanation: The exterior is a homotopy pushout over the bottom surfaces.
- **claim-chamber-homotopy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-homotopy-type)): With the markings induced by the preceding construction, $$\label{ch:group-and-spheres} H:=\pi_1D=\pi_1X*F(z_1,\ldots,z_k),\qquad D\simeq BH\vee\bigvee^{2k}S^2,$$ where $BH$ is a finite graph and $z_j$ is the normal-circle generator of the $j$th replacement. On the common surgery-link exterior in the boundary, killing the $z_j$ recovers the original marking into $\pi_1X$.
  - Explanation: Free fundamental group and a graph wedge spheres.
- **claim-exterior-duals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-exterior-duals)): There are proper framed immersed disks $f_j$ in $E$, bounded by the truncated $a_j$-branch curves, and framed spheres $g_j$ in $E$ such that $$\label{ch:dual-pairings} \lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij},\qquad \lambda(g_i,g_j)=0 \quad\text{over }\mathbb Z[\pi_1E].$$ Each $g_j$ is embedded with trivial normal bundle. If all terminal caps selected for the branch compressions are mutually disjoint, these representatives have exactly one geometric intersection between $f_j$ and $g_j$ and no other intersections.
  - Explanation: Framed disks and spheres pair as algebraic duals.
- **claim-poincare-chamber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-poincare-chamber)): The manifold $D$ has a specified framed immersed hyperbolic basis for its full module $\pi_2D$, with vanishing quadratic self-intersections. Attach one three-cell on each of these $2k$ basis spheres, and call the resulting space $P$. Then $(P,S)$ is an oriented finite four-dimensional Poincaré pair, the inclusion $(D,S)\to(P,S)$ preserves the fundamental group and the boundary, and $P\simeq BH$.
  - Explanation: A hyperbolic basis yields an oriented Poincare pair.
- **claim-boundary-surjective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-boundary-surjectivity)): The boundary marking $\pi_1S\to H$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: The boundary marking is surjective.
- **claim-cover-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-cover-data)): For every $d$, the data $(D_d,P_d,S_d,H_d)$ satisfy Propositions <a href="#ch:homotopy-type" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ch:homotopy-type">38</a> and <a href="#ch:poincare-chamber" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ch:poincare-chamber">40</a> with all the bodies and basis spheres lifted. In particular, $S_d$ is connected, $H_d$ is free, $P_d\simeq BH_d$, and $(P_d,S_d)$ is the oriented Poincaré pair obtained by attaching three-cells to a specified framed immersed hyperbolic basis of $D_d$.
  - Explanation: Chamber data persist in every cyclic cover.
- **claim-even-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-even-filling)): The boundary $S_2$ has a marked filling $D'_2$. It can be obtained from $D_2$ by replacing disjoint punctured $S^2\times S^2$ neighbourhoods by four-balls. In particular, there is a homotopy equivalence $(D'_2,S_2)\to(P_2,S_2)$ equal to the identity on the boundary.
  - Explanation: The double cover boundary has a marked filling.
- **claim-endpoint-assignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-assignment)): Let $K$ have characteristic zero, and let $\Gamma$ be a finite loopless multigraph with distinguished endpoints as above. For each edge $e$, choose a coordinate block $h_e$ of dimension $N_e\geq0$ and functions $F_e,b_e\in K\langle h_e\rangle$. For each vertex $v$, choose a family $\mathcal L_v$ of nonempty subsets of $E(v)$, and set $$\begin{aligned} T_v&=K[t_e:e\in E_+(v)]/(t_e^2:e\in E_+(v)),\label{ten:parameters}\\ \mathcal S_v(h,t) &=\sum_{e\in E_+(v)}(F_e(h_e)+t_e b_e(h_e)) -\sum_{e\in E_-(v)}F_e(h_e).\label{ten:vertex-potential} \end{aligned}$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$M_v(y_v,t)\in (y_v,t)T_v[[y_v]]^{\sum_{e\in E(v)}N_e}, \qquad y_v=(y_{v,1},\ldots,y_{v,a_v}),$$ satisfying the following conditions.* 1. *The central tangent maps $A_v=\partial_{y_v}M_v(0,0)$ are injective, and the difference map $$\label{ten:transversality} \begin{split} \Lambda:\bigoplus_{v\in V}K^{a_v}&\longrightarrow \bigoplus_{e\in E}K^{N_e},\\ (\xi_v)_v&\longmapsto \bigl((A_{v_+(e)}\xi_{v_+(e)})_e -(A_{v_-(e)}\xi_{v_-(e)})_e\bigr)_e \end{split}$$ is an isomorphism.* 2. *One has $\mathcal S_v(M_v(y_v,t),t)=0$ identically for every vertex $v$.* 3. *For every $L\in\mathcal L_v$, put $I=L\cap E_+(v)$ and $J=L\cap E_-(v)$. Then $$\label{ten:law-membership} \left(\prod_{i\in I}t_i\right) \left(\prod_{j\in J}b_j\right)(M_v) \in \bigl((\partial_h\mathcal S_v)(M_v)\bigr) \subset T_v[[y_v]].$$ The ideal on the right is generated by all restricted internal partial derivatives of $\mathcal S_v$.* *There is an endpoint assignment $a:E\to V$ such that, for every $v\in V$ and $L\in\mathcal L_v$, at least one edge of $L$ is assigned to its endpoint other than $v$.
  - Explanation: Formal data force an endpoint assignment avoiding every law set.
- **claim-odd-cycle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-odd-cycle)): Let $d\geq3$ be odd. On the cycle with vertices $\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z$, replace every neighboring pair by five red and five blue parallel edges. Distinguish the two endpoints of each edge arbitrarily. At each vertex let $\mathcal L_v$ consist of every four-element subset of incident edges containing two red and two blue edges. There are no data satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#ten:assignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ten:assignment">45</a> for this graph and these law families.
  - Explanation: No such data exist on the colored odd cycle.
- **claim-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-specialization)): If data satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#ten:assignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ten:assignment">45</a> exist, the same algebraic identities, ideal memberships, and tangent conditions can be realized over a finite field of characteristic $q$, for arbitrarily large primes $q$, with the same graph and law families. The functions $F_e,b_e$ remain algebraic and depend only on their own edge block.
  - Explanation: Data specialize to finite fields of large characteristic.
- **claim-graph-forms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-graph-forms)): Let $c_1,\ldots,c_r$ be graph equations for a smooth formal graph over one of the square-free parameter rings, centered modulo its parameter ideal. In the truncated differential forms of its ambient space, set $$\label{ten:graph-form} U_c=\bigwedge_{s=1}^r c_s^{q-1}\mathrm dc_s.$$ Then $\mathrm dU_c=0$; the graph ideal annihilates $U_c$; and every internal one-form restricting to zero on the graph wedges to zero with $U_c$.
  - Explanation: The graph form is closed and annihilated by the graph ideal.
- **claim-nonzero-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-nonzero)): The total contraction of $(\omega_v)_{v\in V}$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: The total contraction is nonzero.
- **claim-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-projection)): Let $(H,\delta)$ be a finite-dimensional graded complex over a field and let $K_0\subset H$ be a graded subspace. There is a chain projection $P:H\to H$, chain-homotopic to the identity, whose image lies in $\ker\delta$ and maps isomorphically onto $H(H,\delta)$, and such that $P(K_0)\subset K_0\cap\ker\delta$.
  - Explanation: A chain projection to cycles preserves a chosen subspace.
- **claim-kernel-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-lift)): Let $x\in\mathcal K_e\cap\ker\delta_e$ be homogeneous and let $a$ be a homogeneous $D_e$-cycle representing $x$. There is a form $a'$ of the same exterior degree such that $$\label{ten:lift-equation} (D_e+t\,\mathrm db_e\wedge(-))(a+ta')=0 \quad\text{in }\Omega_e[t]/(t^2).$$
  - Explanation: Kernel cycles lift over the square-zero parameter ring.
- **claim-odd-nonfilling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-odd-nonfilling)): For every odd integer $d\geq3$, the marked boundary $S_d$ admits no marked filling.
  - Explanation: Odd covers of degree at least three admit no marked filling.
- **claim-marked-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-cuts)): A marked filling of $S_d$ gives a compact connected topological four-manifold $W$ with boundary identified with $\partial X_d$ and with the marked graph homotopy type of $X_d$. It admits a decomposition over $\Gamma_d$ with connected vertex pieces $V_v$ and connected three-dimensional cuts $Y_e$ such that:* 1. *the outside boundary face of $V_v$ is the prescribed $K_v$, and $\partial Y_e$ is precisely the prescribed join torus;* 2. *every marked four-slot law at $v$ bounds a disk in $V_v$;* 3. *the cuts are compact smooth three-manifolds, and the filled vertex pieces are smooth away from finitely many interior points.* *The identifications retain the based port meridians, their longitudes, and the named extra loops.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical filling decomposes into vertex pieces and cuts.
- **claim-linear-cut-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-linear)): The map $\Delta^2$ is an isomorphism. The map $\Delta^1$ is surjective and restricts to an isomorphism on the subspace where evaluation on every named extra loop is zero. For each cut $Y_e$, the image of $H^1(Y_e)$ in $H^1(\partial Y_e)$ is one of the two original coordinate lines.
  - Explanation: Restriction maps are isomorphisms or surjections as stated.
- **claim-formal-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-formal-data)): For the cut pieces of Lemma <a href="#for:cuts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="for:cuts">53</a>, there are a characteristic-zero field $K$, coordinate blocks $h_e=(h_{e,1},\ldots,h_{e,N_e})$, and algebraic power-series germs $F_e(h_e),b_e(h_e)$ over $K$, with $F_e(0)=b_e(0)=0$, as follows. For each vertex set $$\label{for:vertex-potentials} \begin{split} T_v&=K[t_e:e\in E_+(v)]/(t_e^2:e\in E_+(v)),\\ \mathcal S_v&=\sum_{e\in E_+(v)}(F_e+t_eb_e) -\sum_{e\in E_-(v)}F_e. \end{split}$$ There are smooth formal graph parametrizations $M_v(y_v,t)$ in the incident $h_e$ blocks over $T_v$, centered modulo $(t)$, such that:* 1. *their central tangent maps are injective, and the difference map from the sum of the vertex tangent spaces to the sum of the edge blocks is an isomorphism;* 2. *$\mathcal S_v(M_v)=0$;* 3. *for $L\in\mathcal L_v$, with $I=L\cap E_+(v)$ and $J=L\cap E_-(v)$, $$\label{for:mixed-membership} \left(\prod_{i\in I}t_i\right) \left(\prod_{j\in J}b_j\right)(M_v) \in\bigl((\partial_h\mathcal S_v)(M_v)\bigr) \subset T_v[[y_v]].$$* *Empty products equal one. In every parameter ring, only the individual squares $t_e^2$ vanish; mixed products are retained. One can take $K=\mathbb C((\sigma))$.
  - Explanation: The cuts give formal data meeting the tensor theorem hypotheses.
- **claim-edge-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-edge-input)): Let $Y_e$ be compact, connected, oriented and smooth, with connected torus boundary and based coordinate slopes $\alpha_e,\beta_e$. Suppose the image of $H^1(Y_e;\mathbb C)$ on the boundary detects $\alpha_e$ and vanishes on $\beta_e$. With internal coordinates $h_e$ on $\mathcal E_e$ and a scalar parameter $\tau^2=0$, there is a formal family $s_e(h_e,\tau)$ of connection one-forms, centered at the trivial connection, and formal functions $f_e\in(h_e)^3$, $b_e^{\mathrm{form}}\in(h_e)^2$ such that:* 1. *The boundary trace is $\tau T_e\zeta_e+v_e(h_e,\tau)\xi_e$. The internal gradient ideal of $f_e+\tau b_e^{\mathrm{form}}$ equals the full curvature ideal of $s_e(h_e,\tau)$ in $\mathbb C[[h_e]][\tau]/(\tau^2)$.* 2. *Its quotient represents, over every local Artinian coefficient algebra, flat connections modulo based gauges with $G_{\alpha_e}=\exp(-\tau T_e)$; equivalently, it is the formal germ of based representations of $\pi_1Y_e$ with that condition. On this quotient $G_{\beta_e}=\exp(-v_e(h_e,\tau))$. Once a flat connection has the displayed torus trace, its gauge into this family is equal to identity on the whole torus.* 3. *At $\tau=0$, writing $G_{\beta_e}$ for the marked holonomy on the central critical scheme, one has $$\label{for:edge-log-coefficient} b_e^{\mathrm{form}} =-\epsilon_e\operatorname{tr}(T_e\log G_{\beta_e}) \pmod{\operatorname{Jac}(f_e)}.$$
  - Explanation: A single cut gives a formal slice with gradient ideal equal to curvature ideal.
- **claim-vertex-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-for-vertex-identities)): The flat edge restrictions lift to a formal parametrization $M_v^{\mathrm{form}}$ over $R_v$ satisfying $$\label{for:square-gradient} \mathcal S_v^{\mathrm{form}}(M_v^{\mathrm{form}})\in\mathcal I_v^2, \qquad (\partial_h\mathcal S_v^{\mathrm{form}})(M_v^{\mathrm{form}}) =L_vr_v,$$ where $L_v$ has a full-column minor that is a unit at the joint origin. With $\sigma$ and the $t_e$ fixed, the tangent maps at the joint origin are the restriction maps in <a href="#for:tangent-isomorphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="for:tangent-isomorphism">[for:tangent-isomorphism]</a>.
  - Explanation: Vertex sections have potential in the square of the flatness ideal.

        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold with connected boundary $S$ and free fundamental group $H$.       "explanation": "Chamber data with a filling in the double cover and none in odd covers.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-refl-chamber-data",
          "exact_quote": "For $d\\geq1$, put $H_d=w^{-1}(d\\mathbb Z)$, let $D_d,P_d$ be the associated connected cyclic covers, and let $S_d$ be the preimage of $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-320.1"
        }
      ],

      "exact_statement": "Let $W(\\mathcal G)$ be a right-angled Coxeter group on an arbitrary simple graph with vertex set $V$. This support is finite, and graph automorphisms transport supports.",
      "explanation": "Special subgroups have the intersection formula and unique finite supports.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-outer-actions",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#ext:outer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:outer\">[ext:outer]</a>, the action of $\\mathcal E/\\Pi$ on $Q_2$ is the label-translation and cyclic-deck action $$(a,t)[b,y]=[a+b,\\theta^t y],\n \\qquad (a,t)\\in\\mathsf A\\times\\mathbb F_2.$$ With the markings supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#mod:models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:models\">14</a>, the graph action on $M_\\epsilon$ from Proposition <a href=\"#mod:actions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:actions\">16</a> induces exactly the subgroup $$\\mathsf B_\\epsilon\n   =\\{(a,\\epsilon(a)):a\\in\\mathsf A\\}\n   \\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Out}}(\\Pi).$$ In particular, every element of $\\mathsf A=\\mathsf B_0$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $M$, and every element of $\\mathsf B_\\ell$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-320.1"

      "exact_statement": "Under <a href=\"#ext:outer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:outer\">[ext:outer]</a>, the action of $\\mathcal E/\\Pi$ on $Q_2$ is the label-translation and cyclic-deck action $$(a,t)[b,y]=[a+b,\\theta^t y],\n \\qquad (a,t)\\in\\mathsf A\\times\\mathbb F_2.$$ With the markings supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#mod:models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:models\">14</a>, the graph action on $M_\\epsilon$ from Proposition <a href=\"#mod:actions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:actions\">16</a> induces exactly the subgroup $$\\mathsf B_\\epsilon\n   =\\{(a,\\epsilon(a)):a\\in\\mathsf A\\}\n   \\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Out}}(\\Pi).$$ In particular, every element of $\\mathsf A=\\mathsf B_0$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $M$, and every element of $\\mathsf B_\\ell$ is induced by a homeomorphism of $N$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-hyp-cubical-model",
        "exact_quote": "There are a locally finite tree $T$ with a free cocompact $H$-action, an $H$-equivariant family of finite subtrees $(T_u)_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}}$, and a locally finite CAT(0) cube complex $X$ with a proper cocompact $\\mathcal E$-action, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *If $u,u'$ are adjacent in $\\widehat{\\mathcal L}$, then $T_u$ and $T_{u'}$ meet at a vertex. Moreover, $$b_T:=\\sup_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}}|T_u^{(0)}|<\\infty.$$*\n\n\n2.  *The vertices of $X$ are the pairs $(a,t)\\in W\\times T^{(0)}$. Its edges are exactly $$\\begin{aligned}\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(au,t)\n            &&\\text{if }u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}\\text{ and }t\\in T_u^{(0)},\\\\\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(a,t')\n            &&\\text{if }[t,t']\\text{ is an edge of }T.\n          \\end{aligned}\n          \\label{hyp:edge-graph}$$*\n\n3.  *On these vertices the action is $$(b,h)(a,t)=(b\\,h(a),ht)\n          \\qquad (b\\in W,\\ h\\in H).$$ The cubes of $X$ have dimension at most five.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hyp-cubical-model",
          "exact_quote": "There are a locally finite tree $T$ with a free cocompact $H$-action, an $H$-equivariant family of finite subtrees $(T_u)_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}}$, and a locally finite CAT(0) cube complex $X$ with a proper cocompact $\\mathcal E$-action, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *If $u,u'$ are adjacent in $\\widehat{\\mathcal L}$, then $T_u$ and $T_{u'}$ meet at a vertex. Moreover, $$b_T:=\\sup_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}}|T_u^{(0)}|<\\infty.$$*\n\n\n2.  *The vertices of $X$ are the pairs $(a,t)\\in W\\times T^{(0)}$. Its edges are exactly $$\\begin{aligned}\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(au,t)\n            &&\\text{if }u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}\\text{ and }t\\in T_u^{(0)},\\\\\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(a,t')\n            &&\\text{if }[t,t']\\text{ is an edge of }T.\n          \\end{aligned}\n          \\label{hyp:edge-graph}$$*\n\n3.  *On these vertices the action is $$(b,h)(a,t)=(b\\,h(a),ht)\n          \\qquad (b\\in W,\\ h\\in H).$$ The cubes of $X$ have dimension at most five.",
          "source_id": "PM-320.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There are a locally finite tree $T$ with a free cocompact $H$-action, an $H$-equivariant family of finite subtrees $(T_u)_{u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}}$, and a locally finite CAT(0) cube complex $X$ with a proper cocompact $\\mathcal E$-action, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *If $u,u'$ are adjacent in $\\widehat{\\mathcal L}$, then $T_u$ and $T_{u'}$ meet at a vertex. Its edges are exactly $$\\begin{aligned}\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(au,t)\n            &&\\text{if }u\\in\\widehat{\\mathcal V}\\text{ and }t\\in T_u^{(0)},\\\\\n          (a,t)&\\longleftrightarrow(a,t')\n            &&\\text{if }[t,t']\\text{ is an edge of }T.\n          \\end{aligned}\n          \\label{hyp:edge-graph}$$*\n\n3.  *On these vertices the action is $$(b,h)(a,t)=(b\\,h(a),ht)\n          \\qquad (b\\in W,\\ h\\in H).$$ The cubes of $X$ have dimension at most five.",

        "anchor": "stmt-nor-coxeter-facts",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W(\\mathcal G)$ be the right-angled Coxeter group on a simple graph $\\mathcal G$ with vertex set $V$.*\n\n1.  *For $u\\in V$, $$C_{W(\\mathcal G)}(u)=W_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{st}}_{\\mathcal G}(u)}.$$*\n\n2.  *Every finite-order element is conjugate to a product of distinct generators supported on a clique, with the empty product allowed.*\n\n3.  *The center is the special subgroup on the universal vertices: $$Z(W(\\mathcal G))\n     =W_{\\{v\\in V:\\ v\\text{ is adjacent to every }u\\ne v\\}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ch-cover-data",
        "exact_quote": "For every $d$, the data $(D_d,P_d,S_d,H_d)$ satisfy Propositions <a href=\"#ch:homotopy-type\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ch:homotopy-type\">38</a> and <a href=\"#ch:poincare-chamber\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ch:poincare-chamber\">40</a> with all the bodies and basis spheres lifted. In particular, $S_d$ is connected, $H_d$ is free, $P_d\\simeq BH_d$, and $(P_d,S_d)$ is the oriented Poincaré pair obtained by attaching three-cells to a specified framed immersed hyperbolic basis of $D_d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ch-even-filling",
        "exact_quote": "The boundary $S_2$ has a marked filling $D'_2$. It can be obtained from $D_2$ by replacing disjoint punctured $S^2\\times S^2$ neighbourhoods by four-balls. In particular, there is a homotopy equivalence $(D'_2,S_2)\\to(P_2,S_2)$ equal to the identity on the boundary.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-320.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-assignment",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K$ have characteristic zero, and let $\\Gamma$ be a finite loopless multigraph with distinguished endpoints as above. For each edge $e$, choose a coordinate block $h_e$ of dimension $N_e\\geq0$ and functions $F_e,b_e\\in K\\langle h_e\\rangle$. For each vertex $v$, choose a family $\\mathcal L_v$ of nonempty subsets of $E(v)$, and set $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_v&=K[t_e:e\\in E_+(v)]/(t_e^2:e\\in E_+(v)),\\label{ten:parameters}\\\\\n \\mathcal S_v(h,t)\n &=\\sum_{e\\in E_+(v)}(F_e(h_e)+t_e b_e(h_e))\n       -\\sum_{e\\in E_-(v)}F_e(h_e).\\label{ten:vertex-potential}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$M_v(y_v,t)\\in (y_v,t)T_v[[y_v]]^{\\sum_{e\\in E(v)}N_e},\n \\qquad y_v=(y_{v,1},\\ldots,y_{v,a_v}),$$ satisfying the following conditions.*\n\n\n1.  *The central tangent maps $A_v=\\partial_{y_v}M_v(0,0)$ are injective, and the difference map $$\\label{ten:transversality}\n     \\begin{split}\n     \\Lambda:\\bigoplus_{v\\in V}K^{a_v}&\\longrightarrow\n                             \\bigoplus_{e\\in E}K^{N_e},\\\\\n     (\\xi_v)_v&\\longmapsto\n     \\bigl((A_{v_+(e)}\\xi_{v_+(e)})_e\n                 -(A_{v_-(e)}\\xi_{v_-(e)})_e\\bigr)_e\n     \\end{split}$$ is an isomorphism.*\n\n2.  *One has $\\mathcal S_v(M_v(y_v,t),t)=0$ identically for every vertex $v$.*\n\n\n3.  *For every $L\\in\\mathcal L_v$, put $I=L\\cap E_+(v)$ and $J=L\\cap E_-(v)$. Then $$\\label{ten:law-membership}\n     \\left(\\prod_{i\\in I}t_i\\right)\n     \\left(\\prod_{j\\in J}b_j\\right)(M_v)\n     \\in\n     \\bigl((\\partial_h\\mathcal S_v)(M_v)\\bigr)\n                             \\subset T_v[[y_v]].$$ The ideal on the right is generated by all restricted internal partial derivatives of $\\mathcal S_v$.*\n\n*There is an endpoint assignment $a:E\\to V$ such that, for every $v\\in V$ and $L\\in\\mathcal L_v$, at least one edge of $L$ is assigned to its endpoint other than $v$.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ten-assignment",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K$ have characteristic zero, and let $\\Gamma$ be a finite loopless multigraph with distinguished endpoints as above. Then $$\\label{ten:law-membership}\n     \\left(\\prod_{i\\in I}t_i\\right)\n     \\left(\\prod_{j\\in J}b_j\\right)(M_v)\n     \\in\n     \\bigl((\\partial_h\\mathcal S_v)(M_v)\\bigr)\n                             \\subset T_v[[y_v]].$$ The ideal on the right is generated by all restricted internal partial derivatives of $\\mathcal S_v$.*\n\n*There is an endpoint assignment $a:E\\to V$ such that, for every $v\\in V$ and $L\\in\\mathcal L_v$, at least one edge of $L$ is assigned to its endpoint other than $v$.",

Then $$\\label{ten:law-membership}\n     \\left(\\prod_{i\\in I}t_i\\right)\n     \\left(\\prod_{j\\in J}b_j\\right)(M_v)\n     \\in\n     \\bigl((\\partial_h\\mathcal S_v)(M_v)\\bigr)\n                             \\subset T_v[[y_v]].$$ The ideal on the right is generated by all restricted internal partial derivatives of $\\mathcal S_v$.*\n\n*There is an endpoint assignment $a:E\\to V$ such that, for every $v\\in V$ and $L\\in\\mathcal L_v$, at least one edge of $L$ is assigned to its endpoint other than $v$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-graph-forms",
        "exact_quote": "Let $c_1,\\ldots,c_r$ be graph equations for a smooth formal graph over one of the square-free parameter rings, centered modulo its parameter ideal. In the truncated differential forms of its ambient space, set $$\\label{ten:graph-form}\n U_c=\\bigwedge_{s=1}^r c_s^{q-1}\\mathrm dc_s.$$ Then $\\mathrm dU_c=0$; the graph ideal annihilates $U_c$; and every internal one-form restricting to zero on the graph wedges to zero with $U_c$.",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ten-graph-forms",
          "exact_quote": "Let $c_1,\\ldots,c_r$ be graph equations for a smooth formal graph over one of the square-free parameter rings, centered modulo its parameter ideal.           "source_id": "PM-320.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x\\in\\mathcal K_e\\cap\\ker\\delta_e$ be homogeneous and let $a$ be a homogeneous $D_e$-cycle representing $x$. There is a form $a'$ of the same exterior degree such that $$\\label{ten:lift-equation}\n (D_e+t\\,\\mathrm db_e\\wedge(-))(a+ta')=0\n                       \\quad\\text{in }\\Omega_e[t]/(t^2).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-320.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ten-lift",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x\\in\\mathcal K_e\\cap\\ker\\delta_e$ be homogeneous and let $a$ be a homogeneous $D_e$-cycle representing $x$. There is a form $a'$ of the same exterior degree such that $$\\label{ten:lift-equation}\n (D_e+t\\,\\mathrm db_e\\wedge(-))(a+ta')=0\n                       \\quad\\text{in }\\Omega_e[t]/(t^2).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-320.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **aspherical manifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A connected manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible.
- **closed topological manifold model** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *closed topological $n$-manifold model* of a space is a closed, connected topological $n$-manifold equipped with a homotopy equivalence to that space.
- **flag complex** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Here *flag* means that every set of pairwise adjacent vertices spans a simplex.
- **flag-no-square triangulation** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Here *flag-no-square* means flag with no induced four-cycle in the one-skeleton.
- **marked filling** ([source](#stmt-ch-marked-filling)): A marked filling of $S_d$ is a compact oriented topological four-manifold $D'_d$, an orientation-preserving identification $\partial D'_d=S_d$, and a homotopy equivalence $D'_d\to BH_d$ whose boundary restriction is homotopic to the marking $S_d\to P_d\simeq BH_d$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-cyclic-criterion** [claim](#stmt-cyc-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cyc-criterion): Lift a hypothetical homeomorphism to the infinite cyclic cover and compose with a large translation to build odd covers. -> non-realizable outer automorphism
- **r-sylow** [claim](#stmt-sym-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sym-criterion): Sylow conjugacy inside the realized group. -> non-homeomorphic models
- **r-edge-input** [claim](#stmt-for-edge-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-for-vertex-identities): Import the single-cut formal slice. -> edge potential and perturbation
- **r-vertex** [claim](#stmt-for-vertex-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-for-vertex-identities): Stokes boundary calculation and nilpotent correction. -> vertex parametrizations
- **r-odd-cycle** [claim](#stmt-ten-odd-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ten-odd-cycle): Deficient-color alternation on an odd cycle. -> no data on odd cycle
- **r-odd-nonfilling** [claim](#stmt-for-odd-nonfilling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-for-vertex-identities-2): Cut the filling and contradict the odd-cycle obstruction. -> no marked filling
- **r-even-filling** [claim](#stmt-ch-even-filling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-even-filling): Select red caps at one vertex and blue at the other, then replace sphere pairs by balls. -> double cover filling
- **r-chamber-data** [claim](#stmt-refl-chamber-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-refl-chamber-data): Assemble chamber propositions. -> parity chamber
- **r-quotient-odd** [claim](#stmt-mod-odd-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-odd-models): Pull back the finite cover and lift the equivalence. -> no odd models for quotient
- **r-models** [claim](#stmt-mod-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-models): Glue twisted fillings along panels. -> two manifold models
- **r-delta** [claim](#stmt-ext-delta) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-delta): Apply the cyclic criterion. -> delta unrealized
- **r-hyperbolic** [claim](#stmt-hyp-word-hyperbolic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hyp-word-hyperbolic): Grid bound, thin triangles, quasi-isometry. -> word-hyperbolic
- **r-finite-out** [claim](#stmt-hyp-finite-out) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hyp-finite-out): Splitting theorem against no virtually cyclic splitting. -> finite Out
- **r-normalizer** [claim](#stmt-nor-normalizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nor-normalizer): Rigidity of Coxeter automorphisms plus label code rigidity. -> normalizer equality
- **r-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nor-normalizer-2): Verify hypotheses of the Sylow criterion. -> non-homeomorphic pair

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-relation-space-dimension**: For the explicit relation vectors built from one shared marked vertex per pair and pairwise disjoint blocks, the generated subspace has dimension eight, so the eight generators are linearly independent.
- **inv-min-weight**: Every nonzero element of the relation space has weight at least eight; in the explicit construction the smallest weight comes from a single generator.
- **inv-distinct-subset-weights**: All subset sums of the generators have pairwise different weights, which is what forces a weight-preserving coordinate permutation to fix every generator.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-large-simplices**: Assuming the relation space meets the span of a vertex set trivially for vertex sets larger than a simplex of the triangulation. -> The independence argument compares the relation weight bound with the size of a simplex; larger vertex sets can contain nonzero relations, so the quotient labels need not stay independent.
- **anti-small-triangulation**: Running the construction on a triangulation with fewer vertices than the construction needs. -> The disjoint blocks and the tetrahedron cannot all be placed, so the generators cannot be built as specified.
- **anti-overlapping-blocks**: Reusing vertices between the blocks of different generators to save vertices. -> Extra overlaps change the cancellation pattern in subset sums, so the weight formula and distinctness of weights no longer hold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The rigid label relation space is an explicit finite construction over the two-element field; its dimension, minimum weight and subset-sum weight separation can be computed exactly by finite enumeration at the paper's parameters and at smaller prefixes. The rigidity of simplicial automorphisms of the triangulation is not tested here.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer K in 1..8 on stdin, the number of relation vectors c_0..c_{K-1}
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= K <= 8; consecutive pairs share one marked vertex; blocks have size 16*2^i-1 and are pairwise disjoint
# OUTPUTS: one line: dim=<d> minweight=<w> outside=<n> distinct=<0|1>
# TIME: O(K * 2^K * V) bit operations, V the number of vertices used
# SPACE: O(2^K) weights plus K bitsets
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers used as bitsets over the two-element field
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys


def main():
    K = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
    marked = (K + 1) // 2
    c = []
    pos = marked
    for i in range(K):
        size = 16 * (1 << i) - 1
        mask = 1 << (i // 2)
        for s in range(pos, pos + size):
            mask |= 1 << s
        pos += size
        c.append(mask)
    outside = pos - marked
    basis = []
    for v in c:
        for b in basis:
            v = min(v, v ^ b)
        if v:
            basis.append(v)
    dim = len(basis)
    weights = []
    for J in range(1 << K):
        x = 0
        for i in range(K):
            if (J >> i) & 1:
                x ^= c[i]
        weights.append(bin(x).count('1'))
    minw = min(weights[1:])
    distinct = 1 if len(set(weights)) == len(weights) else 0
    print(f"dim={dim} minweight={minw} outside={outside} distinct={distinct}")


main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence statement about topological four-manifolds. It prescribes no finite procedure that a program could run, so there is no software artifact to build from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The manifolds are abstract closed aspherical four-dimensional objects with no metric, dynamics or physics, so they do not give a usable simulation or game-world construction. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The self-map result only rules out realizing a homotopy equivalence by a homeomorphism. It offers no checker, converter or utility that a tool could implement. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only finite combinatorial argument is a parity contradiction on odd colored cycles, used as a nonexistence step inside a proof. It is not a procedure with inputs and outputs worth implementing. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Word-hyperbolicity of the groups is a geometric group-theoretic property. No representation, index or container with operations and costs is defined. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cube complex used in the proof is a proof device of bounded cube dimension. It maps to no hardware dataflow or accelerator workload. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The binary relation space with large minimum weight rigidifies a triangulation symmetry. It is not a code, hardness assumption or protocol with a security claim. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements concern homotopy classes of self-maps of manifolds. There is no data, model, loss or learning guarantee anywhere in the source. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The endpoint-assignment theorem asserts the existence of an assignment under algebraic hypotheses. It gives no objective, constraint solver or bound, so no optimisation model follows. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nonexistence of fillings for odd cyclic covers is a topological obstruction. No nodes, messages, faults or consensus setting appear in the source. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to pure geometric topology and group theory. Its Sylow criterion needs a finite outer automorphism group, and it gives no direct engineering deliverable outside mathematics. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-topological-dimension-four** (formal_scope): The non-homeomorphism and self-map results are proved for closed aspherical topological four-manifolds in the topological category. The source treats homeomorphism only, not diffeomorphism, and says rigidity holds in the other ranges it cites. Blocked use: ['Concluding that homotopy-equivalent aspherical manifolds fail to be homeomorphic in dimensions other than four.', 'Using the pair as an example of exotic smooth structures or of diffeomorphism failure.']
- **limit-existence-not-prescribed-class** (counterexample_scope): Theorem 1 excludes every homeomorphism between the two manifolds without prescribing a homotopy class, so it is not by itself a statement about one specific homotopy equivalence. The source states that a nonhomeomorphic pair alone would not imply the self-map assertion, which is proved separately. Blocked use: ['Deducing from the pair alone that a given self-equivalence of one manifold is not homotopic to a homeomorphism.', 'Reading the pair as a counterexample to the prescribed-class formulation of the Borel conjecture without the separate self-map argument.']
- **limit-cyclic-criterion-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The cyclic-cover criterion needs a finite aspherical CW complex with an epimorphism of its fundamental group onto the integers, a closed manifold model of the double cover, and no model for every sufficiently large odd cover. Without these the outer automorphism is not shown to be non-realizable. Blocked use: ['Applying the criterion to a space whose double cover has no closed manifold model.', 'Applying the criterion when large odd covers are known to have manifold models.']
- **limit-odd-degree-regime** (edge_case): The nonexistence of models and of marked fillings is asserted only for odd degree at least three. The double cover does have a marked filling and a manifold model, so nonexistence cannot be transferred to even degree. Blocked use: ['Applying the odd-cover obstruction to the degree-two cover.', 'Applying the obstruction to degree one, which the statements do not cover.']
- **limit-triangulation-size-and-flagness** (hypothesis): The relation-space and label constructions are carried out on a finite flag-no-square PL triangulation of the boundary three-manifold with at least 5000 vertices. Smaller or non-flag triangulations do not supply the disjoint blocks or the independence of labels on simplices. Blocked use: ['Running the relation-space construction on a triangulation with too few vertices for the disjoint blocks.', 'Using triangulations that are not flag when the lifted-nerve and Coxeter-group arguments are invoked.']
- **limit-relation-space-rigidity** (formal_scope): The relation space has dimension eight and all nonzero elements have weight at least eight, and only simplicial automorphisms of the triangulation preserving it are shown to be trivial. The statement says nothing about non-simplicial symmetries or about other label spaces. Blocked use: ['Claiming rigidity of arbitrary coordinate permutations of the label group that are not simplicial automorphisms.', 'Using the weight bound for vertex sets larger than a simplex without a separate argument.']
- **limit-finite-out-sylow-input** (hypothesis): The Sylow criterion for distinguishing manifold models requires the outer automorphism group of the common group to be finite, together with the normalizer equality and the non-realization of the involution on the first model. Finiteness is established here only for this specific group. Blocked use: ['Invoking the Sylow criterion for groups whose outer automorphism group is not known to be finite.', 'Using the criterion when the two subgroups are conjugate or the involution is realized.']
- **limit-finite-out-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): Finiteness of the outer automorphism group rests on an imported splitting theorem for word-hyperbolic groups with infinite outer automorphism group, combined with the exclusion of virtually cyclic splittings. No explicit automorphism list or bound on its order is produced. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as an algorithm that enumerates the outer automorphism group.', 'Extracting a numerical bound on the order of the outer automorphism group from the statement.']
- **limit-odd-cycle-multiplicity** (edge_case): The colored cycle obstruction is stated for odd length at least three, with five parallel edges of each of two colors between neighbors and the law sets of two red and two blue edges. Even cycles admit alternating deficient colors, and the counting step uses the multiplicity five. Blocked use: ['Applying the contradiction to even cycles.', 'Applying it with fewer parallel edges per color, where the two-vertices-receive-at-most-two count no longer excludes a shared deficient color.']
- **limit-endpoint-assignment-characteristic-zero** (hypothesis): The endpoint-assignment theorem is stated over a field of characteristic zero, for a finite loopless multigraph with algebraic power-series edge data satisfying transversality, vanishing of the vertex potential and the law memberships. It is an existence statement for an assignment, not a procedure for finding it. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to graphs with loops or to data that fail the transversality isomorphism.', 'Treating the assignment as computable from the formal data.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds that are homotopy equivalent but not homeomorphic, disproving the homeomorphism-existence formulation of the Borel conjecture in dimension four. Their common fundamental group is word-hyperbolic. One of the manifolds also has a self-homotopy equivalence not homotopic to a homeomorphism.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-pair](#stmt-thm-pair) | theorem | `thm:pair` | 1 | There exist closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds $M$ and $N$ that are homotopy equivalent but not homeomorphic. |
| [stmt-thm-self-map](#stmt-thm-self-map) | theorem | `thm:self-map` | 2 | There exist a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold $M$ and a homotopy equivalence $f\colon M\to M$ such that no homeomorphism $M\to M$ is homotopic to $f$.* |
| [stmt-cyc-criterion](#stmt-cyc-criterion) | theorem | `cyc:criterion` | 3 | Let $Q$ be a connected finite aspherical CW complex, put $G=\pi_1(Q)$, and let $w\colon G\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z$ be an epimorphism. |
| [stmt-cyc-homotopy-involution](#stmt-cyc-homotopy-involution) | remark | `cyc:homotopy-involution` | - | Remark 4*. The equivalence $f$ in Theorem 3 satisfies $f^2\simeq\operatorname{id}_M$. |
| [stmt-sym-criterion](#stmt-sym-criterion) | proposition | `sym:criterion` | 5 | Let $X$ and $Y$ be connected topological manifolds whose fundamental groups are marked by a group $\Pi$, and suppose that $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ is finite. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A connected manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible. Its fundamental group then determines its homotopy type. The topological Borel conjecture commonly asks whether every homotopy equivalence between closed aspherical manifolds is homotopic to a homeomorphism <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BartelsLueck2012](#ref-BartelsLueck2012), [Lueck2010](#ref-Lueck2010)]. The weaker *homeomorphism-existence formulation* asks only whether homotopy-equivalent closed aspherical manifolds are homeomorphic. Our main result disproves this formulation in dimension four.

<a id="stmt-thm-pair"></a>
<div id="thm:pair" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds $M$ and $N$ that are homotopy equivalent but not homeomorphic. Their common fundamental group is word-hyperbolic.*

</div>

The conclusion excludes every homeomorphism between the two manifolds, without prescribing a homotopy class. The construction also yields a separate obstruction for a self-map of one manifold.

<a id="stmt-thm-self-map"></a>
<div id="thm:self-map" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *There exist a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold $M$ and a homotopy equivalence $f\colon M\to M$ such that no homeomorphism $M\to M$ is homotopic to $f$.*

</div>

We prove this assertion separately and use the same $M$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:pair">1</a>. A nonhomeomorphic pair alone would not imply the self-map assertion.

The homeomorphism-existence question already appears in Borel’s letter to Serre of May 2, 1953, phrased for compact manifolds that are classifying spaces <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Borel1953](#ref-Borel1953)]. Rigidity holds classically in dimensions at most two, and for closed orientable aspherical three-manifolds by geometrization and three-dimensional rigidity <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KreckLueck2009](#ref-KreckLueck2009), Theorem 0.7 and Section 5]. In higher dimensions, Hsiang and Wall proved the homeomorphism-existence statement for homotopy tori in dimensions at least five <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HsiangWall1969](#ref-HsiangWall1969)], and Farrell and Jones established it for closed negatively curved manifolds in those dimensions <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[FarrellJones1989](#ref-FarrellJones1989)]. Bartels and Lück proved the prescribed-class formulation in dimensions at least five for a class of groups containing word-hyperbolic groups and groups acting geometrically on finite-dimensional CAT(0) spaces <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BartelsLueck2012](#ref-BartelsLueck2012), Theorem A].

In dimension four, these rigidity theorems use further geometric input. Bartels and Lück’s four-dimensional application assumes that the fundamental group is good in the sense of Freedman <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BartelsLueck2012](#ref-BartelsLueck2012), Proposition 0.3]. For closed aspherical topological four-manifolds with torsion-free word-hyperbolic fundamental group, Khan proves rigidity up to topological $s$-cobordism <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Khan2012](#ref-Khan2012), Theorem 1.22 and Corollary 1.23]; the product theorem for five-dimensional topological $s$-cobordisms in Freedman–Quinn assumes a good fundamental group <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[FreedmanQuinn1990](#ref-FreedmanQuinn1990), Theorem 7.1A]. The word-hyperbolicity in Theorem <a href="#thm:pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:pair">1</a> therefore lies within the group-theoretic range of these rigidity results, while the dimension is essential to their topological conclusion. Recent examples of Davis, Hayden, Huang, Ruberman, and Sunukjian are closed aspherical smooth four-manifolds that are homeomorphic but not diffeomorphic <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DHHRS2026](#ref-DHHRS2026), Theorem 1.1]. Their proof uses Davis reflection and a characteristic-subgroup argument to force a hypothetical diffeomorphism between these manifolds to lift to covers distinguished by the genera of smoothly embedded homologically essential surfaces <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DHHRS2026](#ref-DHHRS2026)]. Our conclusion concerns homeomorphism in the topological category.

The proof uses two companion constructions. The marked tensor obstruction of <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[MT](#ref-MT)] supplies plumbing blocks, marked grope bodies, and algebraic potentials associated to three-dimensional cuts. The reflection and realization results of <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[PD](#ref-PD)] turn the absence of a marked filling into the absence of a closed manifold model for an aspherical Poincaré complex. We also use its universal-development and support arguments. We state the imported results at their points of use and verify their hypotheses for the chambers constructed here. The principal inputs are the marked algebraicity theorem <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MT](#ref-MT), Theorem 5.1], the controlled filling implication <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[PD](#ref-PD), Sections 3–5], and the support construction <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[PD](#ref-PD), Lemma 3.3].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The construction

Start with a finite collared Poincaré chamber $(P,S)$ of dimension four, where $P$ has the homotopy type of a finite graph and its fundamental group has an epimorphism to $\mathbb Z$. Write $S_d$ for the boundary in the connected cyclic cover of degree $d$. A *marked filling* of $S_d$ is a compact four-manifold with this identified boundary and the prescribed graph homotopy type, including the boundary map. The double cover $S_2$ has a marked filling, whereas $S_d$ has none for odd $d\geq3$.

This parity comes from two colors of plumbing caps. Each grope has four terminal caps grouped into two red–blue pairs. In the double cover, choosing red caps at one vertex and blue caps at the other makes the selected sheets disjoint and gives the filling. For odd $d\geq3$, a hypothetical filling of $S_d$ instead produces formal data on a cycle of $d$ vertices with five red and five blue edges between each neighboring pair. The endpoint-assignment theorem would assign every edge to one endpoint so that each vertex receives at most one edge in some color. Choose one such color at each vertex. Adjacent vertices cannot choose the same color, since together they would receive at most two of the five edges of that color between them. The choices would alternate around the cycle, impossible when the cycle is odd.

We formulate the endpoint-assignment theorem for arbitrary finite loopless multigraphs. The cyclic-cover criterion works in every manifold dimension. It converts a double-cover model and eventual nonexistence of odd-cover models into a self-homotopy equivalence not represented by any homeomorphism. The proof composes a lifted homeomorphism with a large deck translation and imposes no finite-order condition on that homeomorphism. This last distinction separates the criterion from finite-group Nielsen realization obstructions such as <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BlockWeinberger2008](#ref-BlockWeinberger2008), Theorem 1.6].

To construct the pair, use the reflection method of Davis <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Davis1983](#ref-Davis1983), [Davis2008](#ref-Davis2008)] in the Poincaré-chamber form of <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[PD](#ref-PD)]. A full reflection of $P$ has no manifold model in its cyclic covers of odd degree $d\geq3$. We quotient its elementary abelian group of chamber labels by a binary relation space that acts freely and forces every simplicial symmetry preserving the relations to be trivial. The quotient is a finite aspherical complex $Q$ with the same odd-cover obstruction.

There are two ways to replace the chambers of $Q_2$ by the filling of $S_2$. In one, every boundary identification is the same. In the other, it is twisted by the double-cover deck involution according to a nonzero linear functional $\ell$ on the label group $\mathsf A$. Both gluings are closed manifold models of $Q_2$. They realize two subgroups of outer automorphisms of the common group $\Pi$: the horizontal subgroup $\mathsf A$ on $M$ and the graph subgroup $\mathsf B_\ell$ on $N$. These lie in $\mathsf A\times\langle\delta\rangle\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$, where $\delta$ is the cyclic involution. The cyclic-cover criterion forbids $\delta$ on $M$ and at the same time proves Theorem <a href="#thm:self-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:self-map">2</a>. The subgroups $\mathsf A$ and $\mathsf B_\ell$ are not conjugate in $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$: they contain different numbers of elements admitting finite-order lifts to $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Pi)$.

The remaining step controls all possible homotopy classes of a homeomorphism between the models. We prove that $\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)$ is finite: a uniform bound on square grids makes the support cubulation hyperbolic, and Poincaré duality with the homological Shapiro lemma excludes the virtually cyclic splittings forced by an infinite outer automorphism group. A direct rigidity argument for the right-angled Coxeter group of the infinite lifted triangulation then gives $$N_{\mathop{\mathrm{Out}}(\Pi)}(\mathsf A)=\mathsf A\times\langle\delta\rangle.$$ The normalizer equality and the absence of $\delta$ make $\mathsf A$ a Sylow $2$-subgroup of the outer automorphisms realized on $M$. A homeomorphism from $M$ to $N$ would transport $\mathsf B_\ell$ into that realized group and force the forbidden conjugacy. This proves Theorem <a href="#thm:pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:pair">1</a>.

Section <a href="#sec:cyclic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cyclic">2</a> gives the two realization criteria. Section <a href="#sec:reflection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reflection">3</a> states the chamber data and derives the reflected odd-cover obstruction. Sections <a href="#mod:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:section">4</a> and <a href="#ext:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:section">5</a> construct the two models and identify their outer actions. Sections <a href="#hyp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:section">6</a> and <a href="#nor:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nor:section">7</a> prove the group-theoretic statements and the main theorem. Finally, Sections <a href="#sec:chambers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:chambers">8</a>–<a href="#sec:formal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:formal">10</a> prove the chamber data, including the general tensor theorem and the odd-cover obstruction.

All manifolds are connected unless otherwise indicated. Boundaries have collars, and cornered constructions are rounded. Homotopies of markings are part of the data when boundary maps are prescribed. We use singular cohomology unless a differential-form model is specified, and write $\Gamma_j F$ for the lower central series of a group $F$.

<a id="sec:cyclic"></a>
### Cyclic covers and realized symmetries

Two criteria organize the passage from a filling obstruction to the homeomorphism conclusions. The first forbids a prescribed outer automorphism from being induced by a homeomorphism. The second uses finite symmetry groups to distinguish two manifold models without prescribing the homotopy class of a possible homeomorphism.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### A criterion from cyclic covers

We first isolate the passage from manifold existence in finite covers to a homotopy equivalence that cannot be represented by a homeomorphism. This passage is independent of the four-dimensional construction. A *closed topological $n$-manifold model* of a space is a closed, connected topological $n$-manifold equipped with a homotopy equivalence to that space.

<a id="stmt-cyc-criterion"></a>
<div id="cyc:criterion" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Cyclic-cover criterion). *Let $Q$ be a connected finite aspherical CW complex, put $G=\pi_1(Q)$, and let $w\colon G\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z$ be an epimorphism. For every positive integer $d$, write $$G_d=w^{-1}(d\mathbb Z)
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 Q_d\longrightarrow Q$$ for the associated connected cyclic cover. Fix an integer $n\geq1$. Suppose that $Q_2$ has a closed topological $n$-manifold model $M$, but that $Q_d$ has no such model for every sufficiently large odd integer $d$.*

*Choose $\gamma\in G$ with $w(\gamma)=1$, and use the model equivalence to identify $\pi_1(M)$ with $G_2$. The outer automorphism of $G_2$ represented by $$\alpha(g)=\gamma g\gamma^{-1}$$ is induced by a self-homotopy equivalence of $M$. No homeomorphism of $M$ induces this outer automorphism. In particular, that self-homotopy equivalence is not homotopic to a homeomorphism.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cyc-criterion"><summary>Proof of stmt-cyc-criterion</summary>

*Proof.* The manifold $M$ is aspherical, since it is homotopy equivalent to $Q_2$. Topological manifolds have CW homotopy type <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Milnor1959](#ref-Milnor1959)]. The classification of maps between Eilenberg–Mac Lane spaces therefore realizes $\alpha$ by a map $f\colon M\to M$, and realizes its inverse by a homotopy inverse to $f$; see <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Hatcher2002](#ref-Hatcher2002), Proposition 1B.9].

Suppose that a homeomorphism $h\colon M\to M$ induces the outer class of $\alpha$. We will construct closed manifold models for all sufficiently large odd-degree covers of $Q$. Put $K=\ker w$, and let $$X=K\backslash\widetilde M$$ be the infinite cyclic cover of $M$, where $\widetilde M$ is its universal cover. We use left deck actions throughout. The element $\gamma^2\in G_2$ induces a deck transformation $T$ of $X$, and $T$ generates its deck group over $M$.

<a id="cyc:exact-lift"></a>
<a id="cyc:commutation"></a>
Choose a lift $u\colon\widetilde M\to\widetilde M$ of $h$ that intertwines the deck action by exactly $\alpha$: $$\label{cyc:exact-lift}
 u g u^{-1}=\gamma g\gamma^{-1}
 \qquad(g\in G_2).$$ Indeed, any lift induces an automorphism in the prescribed outer class. Composing that lift with a deck transformation removes the inner discrepancy. Since $\alpha(K)=K$, the map $u$ descends to a homeomorphism $h'\colon X\to X$. Moreover, $\alpha(\gamma^2)=\gamma^2$, so <a href="#cyc:exact-lift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cyc:exact-lift">[cyc:exact-lift]</a> gives $$\label{cyc:commutation}
 h'T=Th'.$$

We next show that a sufficiently large translation composed with $h'$ acts freely and cocompactly on $X$. Represent the homomorphism $w/2\colon G_2\to\mathbb Z$ by a map $M\to S^1$ and lift that map to obtain a continuous height function $$\tau\colon X\longrightarrow\mathbb R,
 \qquad \tau(Tx)=\tau(x)+1.$$ This height is proper. To see this, choose a compact set $C\subset X$ whose $T$-translates cover $X$, using compactness of $M$, and choose $a,b\in\mathbb R$ with $\tau(C)\subset[a,b]$. A bounded closed height band $\tau^{-1}([A,B])$ is a closed subset of $$\bigcup_{\substack{j\in\mathbb Z\\ A-b\leq j\leq B-a}}T^jC,$$ which is a finite union of compact sets.

<a id="cyc:height-increment"></a>
By <a href="#cyc:commutation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cyc:commutation">[cyc:commutation]</a>, the continuous function $x\mapsto\tau(h'x)-\tau(x)$ is $T$-invariant. It is therefore bounded, say in absolute value by $c\geq0$. For an integer $r>c+1$, put $$F_r=T^rh'.$$ For every $x\in X$ we have $$\label{cyc:height-increment}
 0<r-c\leq\tau(F_rx)-\tau(x)\leq r+c.$$ Consequently no nonzero power of $F_r$ fixes a point. Iterating the lower bound shows that only finitely many translates of any compact set can meet that set; thus the $\mathbb Z$-action generated by $F_r$ is properly discontinuous. Finally, along every orbit the height tends to $+\infty$ in positive time and to $-\infty$ in negative time. The first nonnegative height on such an orbit belongs to $[0,r+c]$, by the upper bound in <a href="#cyc:height-increment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cyc:height-increment">[cyc:height-increment]</a>.
